## STANDING RULES (from the ruling)
One seed s4; push only at cell boundaries; every new switch defaults OFF with light checks (off = byte-identical, registry sweep; on changes at least one own decision on a dry replay); expectation and reading committed BEFORE each cell and never changed; readouts with question-clustered 95% CIs, residency-based cold, loss decomposition (wrong / rejected / late engine / late dispatch), absolute and relative D280 FAIR share (state if the re-implementation); no fitted constants or thresholds, no bar or scenario changes, no dropped data, no new datasets (such a fix is ESCALATED and skipped); implementation-bug fixes do not count toward the cap, mechanism or design fixes do; cap 3 fix iterations per step, then "not achieved, likely not the right fix" and the best variant's switch stays OFF unless it met no-harm; STOP and leave for the user: any D240 exception, held-out seeds, freeze, bar changes, escalated fixes, anything INVALID, disk or infra failure I cannot fix; keep the GPU busy (D258 if a build is not ready).

## STEP 1 (P fix + 0b + B): PRE-REGISTERED EXPECTATION AND READING (committed 2026-11-26T23:53Z, before cells (a), (b), (c) and the -er n16 1x, n20, n32 cells; never to be changed)
**Expectation (the user's, verbatim in substance):** n16 1x no harm (cbd >= rung 3 - 2 AND the cloud on-time difference CI includes 0 or is positive); under pressure (halved, n20, n32) cbd >= rung 3 AND FAIR share higher; B net non-negative (right rejects >= wrong rejects, B's own cbd effect >= 0). **Reading rule:** each condition is read at the point estimate for cbd and FAIR share, and on the question-clustered 95% CI for cloud on-time; "met" requires all conditions of that cell; B's net comes from the new per-decision log (la_decisions.jsonl.gz) joined to the per-opportunity outcome against the rung 3 comparator for the same opportunity. Cell-specific: (a) the isolating arm `-0b-er` has no B, so only the P + 0b readout applies (cbd, cloud on-time, cloud-correct, residency-cold with declines separated from made-warm, dashcam, live P accuracy on recall and piece separately); (b) `-la-er` and the n16 1x, n20, n32 `-la-er` cells use the full expectation; (c) the rung 3 replicate reports rung 3 against rung 3 differences with question-clustered CIs on cbd, cloud on-time, residency-cold and dashcam (run-to-run noise). **Comparators:** rung 3 committed cell of the same condition (halved: the `_preD249` cell; n20 and n32: the committed B1 / D301 cells; n16 1x: the committed cell) and the earlier `-la` cell. **Note on the n32 cell of the earlier -la run (step 1 cell 4, no P fix):** its scoring was ordered before this expectation was committed; it is reported with the same readout but is NOT a pre-registered test of this expectation. **If B fails (any pressure cell misses, or n16 1x harms):** iterate B as VALUE-AWARE admission (send iff P(on time) x (cloud accuracy - device accuracy) exceeds the harm to other requests; P(on time) from the estimate's error distribution measured within the run; no fitted constant); if B still fails after the cap of 3 mechanism iterations, drop B and carry P + 0b forward. Loss decomposition categories: wrong (answered, incorrect), rejected (declined by b7 or B), late engine (sent, first token or completion past the deadline), late dispatch (sent late after the opportunity).

**PART P: vLLM 0.31.0 HOST-TIER RULE READ FROM THE SOURCE (own file:line checks; no probe needed for the rule):** (a) capacity: bytes = S x 2^30 (config/vllm.py:1147); num_chunks = floor(bytes / round_up(chunk_bytes, 4096)) (kv_offload/cpu/spec.py:117-120); one chunk = one 16-token block: 3,456 chunks at 13.5 GiB, 6,912 at 27 GiB (the shadow has 3,457 and 6,914: a 0.03 percent gap: NOT the halved-host defect). (b) store: every computed prompt chunk is offered (scheduler.py:660-667, offload_prompt_only); a request with no host load re-offers ALL its blocks, GPU hits included, because next_stored_chunk_idx stays 0 (scheduler.py:1182-1185); a request with a host load offers only blocks after the hit; no store-count admission filter by default (store_threshold 0, spec.py:82-83 and 147). (c) eviction: LRU per chunk (cpu/policies/lru.py); an insert gets a new rank (lru.py:77-83); lookup never refreshes (the scheduler never calls touch); refresh happens only at REQUEST FINISH over all keys the request touched, head first (manager.py:393-420, lru.py:96-104), so the oldest request loses its TAIL first; the offer's own keys are protected from eviction (manager.py:316); if room cannot be found prepare_store returns None and the WHOLE offer is dropped (manager.py:309-319); a host hit copies to GPU and the host copy stays. (d) robot-only rule: max_offload_tokens 0 caps offloadable tokens at 0 (scheduler.py:664-666): nothing stored, host hits still read (repo: offload_policy.py:32,44; predictive_belief.py:292). **Where the shadow differs:** (1) it refreshes at lookup and at start instead of at finish; (2) it inserts the tail first and never touches present chunks; (3) it does not re-offer GPU-hit blocks; (4) it never drops stores. **Could not settle: dropped stores under async pinning (left unmodelled; no constant fitted; a probe (P4 of engine_probe_design.md) would be needed only for this).**

### 2026-11-27 10:11 PST GROUP C PRE-BUILD CHECK (D326 item C): THE SYNC CONTROLLER `link_sync="ours"`: STOPPED AND REPORTED FOR THE USER'S DECISION (no sync-port build started; research agent read of origin; notes scratchpad syncq/NOTES.md)
 1. **Does "ours" order backlog pulls by per-device DEFICIT? NO as a sort key.** The release order is the highest NET MARGINAL VALUE first: `gain = w * (1/gap) * DECISION_PERIOD_S * p_qf * p_ev * (ACC_QF - ACC_NO_EVIDENCE)` (link_sync.py:318) minus `harm = arm._harm_warm(obs, sid, tokens)` (L325); sort `(-net, next-question time, device id)` (L333). The committed FAIR deficit weight `w` (PlannerDeficitWeights, arms_v6cfr3.py:461-464) enters ONLY inside the gain term; there is no deficit-order sort. Fifo mode sorts by reconnect time; the `d177` currency sets gain = 0 (order by -harm only).
 2. **Does it account for load (in-flight queries) before pulling? PARTLY, indirectly, not by a count or threshold gate.** `harm` prices the requests the release would sit in front of (`_queued_behind`, arms_v6state.py:606-660, including in-flight rows when `d249_queue_visibility=True`, which all committed sync arms set; priced by `CO.harm_warm_from_queue_delay`); a release needs net > 0 (L335) or a tie with positive gain, else it is HELD (link_sync_holds_harm); the release is serial (no release while a device is catching up). B's `est_loadaware` is NOT used anywhere in link_sync.py; question-time choices use the queue-free `cf._cf_engine` predictor. `_harm_warm` swallows exceptions and returns 0.0 (L325-327, arms_v6state.py:684-686).
 3. At an outage the emulator holds history batches (`link_sync_hold`); after reconnect live frames upload first and the backlog stays at the device until the coordinator sends a `sync_pull` (whole backlog, oldest first, not rate-limited; one pull per epoch). `_qp` and catch-up prefill are not referenced by link_sync.py (composition untested).
 4. **Composition is a `make_rung3` argument only** (`link_sync`, `link_currency`, `link_tiebreak`, arms_final_ladder.py:484, body 564-566; committed `-q-sync` at arms_final_ladder.py:708 sets `d249_queue_visibility=True, link_sync="ours", link_currency="acc"`); the candidate factory `r3cand` (arms_v6cfr3.py:822-830) does NOT set `d249_queue_visibility`: "cand + sync" would differ from `-q-sync` on that flag unless set deliberately (cand and d249 together not verified).
 5. The driver does not check the arm name for sync (tag `_link_sync` only; arm-side check run_live2_real.py:399-406); `covex-v6-fair-final-cand-tl-sync` would be accepted.
**VERDICT PER YOUR RULE: item (1) is NO and item (2) is only indirect: I STOP here. The sync port (group C), its cells and everything built on it are NOT started; the user decides whether the net-marginal-value order with the deficit weight inside the gain, and the harm-priced queue term, count as deficit and load awareness (then it is composition only), or whether a controller build is wanted.** Group A (rung 3 positive control) and the touch-latch fix proceed independently.

The scorers are elsewhere: `ss2_bench_kit.refspatial_point_accuracy(points, mask)` (verified, line 177) and the exact-match code behind `ss2_analyse.py` / `ss2_compare.py`. (b) The only per-answer SS record is `outcomes.jsonl` `cloud_payload` (`cloud_letter`, `representation`, `tokens_charged`, `on_time`): NO answer text and NO point in `request.jsonl.gz`, `decision.jsonl.gz` or `booking.jsonl.gz`. (c) Every cloud SS answer is a LETTER from the robot recall path (`scores.recall_letter`: the last A-E in the last 300 characters of the reply, else "X"; output_tokens 2 for all 237 asst requests in the checked cell). (d) **REFSPATIAL is unscorable for every arm**: the question asks for a point list and the gold is a mask, the cloud answer is a letter (A 36, B 34, C 48, E 1 in the tallied cell): a letter against a mask would be a substitute metric. (e) **REALWORLDQA is partly recoverable**: of 765 questions 438 are MCQ (the logged letter is a real answer: in 3 audited cells 176 of 215 equal the gold letter, 82% against 33% chance; NOT scored, per the stop instruction) and 327 are free-form (gold is text; only a letter was kept; the letter equals the gold in 0 of about 47 cloud answers in every cell: unscorable). (f) Placeholder check: "X" appears in one cell only (rung 3 s6 plain, 1 answer): not the robot-scorer failure, but refspatial letters and free-form letters are the same kind of artefact. (g) Only the DEVICE side is scored (`assistive_rows.device_score`). To make SS scoring possible a re-run would have to log the SS answer text or the parsed points (with the SS prompt actually sent); scoring MCQ realworldqa alone by letter equality is possible now but leaves free-form and refspatial blank: A METHODOLOGICAL CHOICE FOR THE USER, not made. The SS on-time numbers in every report are ON-TIME DELIVERY, not correctness.
**(2) DASHCAM AP IS NOT A BUG: A VALID QUANTITY WITH A NO-SKILL LEVEL OF ABOUT 0.006 (not 0.44).** Code: `scorer/score.py dashcam_value` -> `_ap_streams` -> `metrics.streaming_ap` -> `study_e9b_dense.ApEngine`. Unit: per GRID POINT (0.25 s grid), not per play; each completed play is a score stream (calibrated cloud score where the answer was on time (1 s), else the calibrated device score, held forward one point). Keys 0.0 / 0.5 / 1.0 / 1.5 = horizon h in seconds BEFORE the event. Positives: one value per positive play (the stream at the grid point nearest event minus h): 53 values; negatives: EVERY grid point of every negative play pooled (9,309 points from 67 plays); no-skill = 53 / (53 + 9,309) = 0.0057 (the 53-of-120 play prevalence does not set it): the committed 0.01 to 0.05 is about 2x to 7x chance. **Recomputed by hand from outcomes.jsonl, the committed StudyORIN1 device scores and the committed calibration for two cells and EQUAL to the committed values:** rung 3 s4 outage 0.04007 / 0.01479 / 0.009923 / 0.009929; cand-tl s4 outage 0.040414 / 0.016609 / 0.011081 / 0.01023 (h = 0, 0.5, 1, 1.5). One inferred input: the trace files are not on this machine, so `play_completed` was inferred and one play (`dash000|p023|02075.mp4`) had to be forced completed to reach 120 plays (53 positive, 67 negative); with it both cells match exactly (the other candidate gave 0.040076 at h = 0). Committed 300-clip comparison (StudyE9b-dense, `results/dashcam/study_e9b/e9b_study1_results.json`, 150 pos / 150 neg clips, the same 0.25 s grid, the same 4B-I device and 32B-I cloud, chance 0.00728, the same AP engine): device-only (zero latency) 0.0284 / 0.0189 / 0.0131 / 0.0105; always-cloud 32B-I 0.0530 / 0.0415 / 0.0166 / 0.0137; the cell's `device_only` AP is 0.0209 / 0.0169 / 0.0122 / 0.0110 (120 plays): the same quantity at a different n; play-level AP carries the D85 caveat (not comparable to the 300-clip AP; plays are not independent). The value people expect (about 0.6 to 0.67) is the StudyEARLY1 / StudyDSMAP challenge mAP: a DIFFERENT metric (one score per clip per horizon): must not be compared with this one. ### 2026-11-28 08:25 PST (box clock about 08:20) D346 COORD-RX: CODE DIAGNOSIS (from the committed cells and the harness code; nothing run on the H100 yet; note research/study_d346_coordrx_diagnosis.md; staged fix NOT applied)
**ROOT CAUSE (supported, exact mechanism NOT yet proven): the crash is the PARENT's 180 s drain running out on a CHILD that has fallen far behind; the child did not deadlock and SUBMISSIONS KEPT GOING (this corrects the H100 reading that the submission path blocked).** (1) Release decisions continue at a steady 820 to 870 per minute (n20) and about 1,050 per minute (n24) up to event time 750 to 963 s; request ROWS are written only when a request completes (`coordinator.py:2261ff`) or as 'abandoned' at finish (`procsplit.py:509-510`), so 'last submission at 277 to 488 s' is the latest submission whose answer came back before the kill (last logged requests took 825 to 1,023 s and finished at 1,202 to 1,378 s, when the child was killed); 7,756 (simplestream) and 8,402 (mooncake) release decisions have no row. `submit_live2` is non-blocking (`asyncbackend.py:131-132`), no semaphore. (2) These arms offer about 13.8 req/s against about 5 to 6 req/s served (every opportunity is released to the cloud with no in-flight cap); TTFT p50 3, 86, 166, 298, 561 s over minutes 0 to 5; the engine counters advance to about 1,450 s (the engine is alive). (3) From about minute 4 the CHILD PROCESS IS SATURATED (contrast cells llf, confidence-cascade n24, mooncake `_c3-11-2`): first token to stream end for 2-token answers 1.5 s then 50, 187, 227 to 283 s (the client is not reading its streams); wire time minus decision time 12 to 29 s by minutes 6 to 8 [0.4 s max in the contrast cells]; the parent's send backlog to the child 863 to 10,838 messages [37 to 49]; late in the run the engine sits idle for 30 to 100 s stretches while thousands of requests are open (the queue has moved into the client). LEADING SUSPECT for the child's CPU/GIL saturation: `_collect` walks every in-flight future (`coordinator.py:2263`) and rebuilds the list (`2413`) on every event and tick (`bridge.py:62,73`) and on every 5 ms self-tick (`procsplit.py:486-489`): at 2,000 to 7,000 in flight roughly 0.25 to 0.7 core-seconds per second (an estimate); a `py-spy --gil` profile decides. **THE CRASH MECHANISM (H1, confirmed, a CONSEQUENCE not the root):** `close` waits 180 s (`procsplit.py:217-239`), then closes the socket with unread data in it, so the child's rx sees ECONNRESET (`procsplit.py:390 -> 94 -> 86`); `__exit__` then waits 60 s and `terminate()`s the child (`procsplit.py:593-596`) before it handles `finish`: no result file and no abandoned rows; flags at `run_live2_real.py:590-596` and `649-656` (`parent_closed_before_result` = the child's file exists but no result came over the socket; `child_never_sent_result` = no file at all); wall 1,534 to 1,573 s = start-up + 1,200 + emulator drain + 180 + 60. REFUTED: H3 (arm-specific deadlock; the decision thread decides at full rate; the crashed arms share only 'release every opportunity to the cloud with no in-flight cap': llf peaks at 8 in flight, cascade peaked at 714 and survived) and H2 (a limit: the httpx limit is 16,384 (`asyncbackend.py:105-107`) and is never reached; no PoolTimeout / ReadTimeout / EMFILE; the stop time varies and tracks latency; the 9 to 29 `ReadError('')` rows also occur in clean cells); H4 (OOM) not needed (the parent's SIGTERM explains the death; the H100 can confirm exit code -15 and no OOM line).
**CORRECTIONS TO EARLIER ENTRIES:** (a) the SimpleStream n20 s4 first cell's coordinator_result.json is from a DIFFERENT attempt (pid 2571456, wall_s 386.5, n_requests 5,017; its host_counters log has two segments about 61,000 s apart): the driver's `mkdir -p $d` (v48 line 364) never clears a directory, so that cell's `parent_closed_before_result`, n_requests 5,017 and n_errors 6 are WRONG (its rows show 29 ReadErrors); a driver guard (refuse or clear a non-empty run dir) is recommended, NOT staged. (b) NOT only `_L3` baseline cells: the same signature also in `mooncake_n32_s4_c3-11-2_L3_epg_rq30` (the FIRST run: a 293 B coordinator_result.json stub; its `_rep2` has an 8,759 B result and is the clean comparator already used) and `mooncake_n16_s4_L3_epg_rq30_out-k3-d120` (child wall 1,379.6 s, backlog 1,184): two earlier statements ('no `_c3-11-2` cell showed it') are corrected: ONE `_c3-11-2` baseline cell (the mooncake n32 FIRST run, never used as a result) showed it; no policy cell and none of the d188 network cells did.
**STAGED FIX (NOT applied; scratchpad coordrx/fix.diff):** `coordinator.py`: each future's done-callback sets a flag and `_collect` skips the walk when nothing has completed since the last one (the prune still runs on every call; dry runs keep the full walk): a skipped walk would have found nothing done, so no decision changes, only CPU time; `run_live2_real.py`: a coordinator_result.json already in the run dir is renamed to `.stale_<mtime>.json` before launch. NO timeout, NO backlog cap, NO `drain_close_s` change. **THIS IS NOT THE USER'S PRE-APPROVED SHAPE (the pre-approved timeout presumes hung streams filling the pool: the diagnosis does NOT show that), and it removes a latency inflation caused by the client, so the scores of SATURATED baseline cells will change (they are crashed, uncited cells today): per the user's rule (any fix that changes what the experiment does stops and comes to the user) THE FIX IS NOT APPLIED; the DIAG decides first (py-spy `--gil` on the child) and the user rules.**
**DIAG RECIPE (staged; pushed as `experiments/orchestration/diag/diag_coordrx.sh`, a NEW path, no existing file changes; GPU 2 only, driver idle, `_DIAG` dir, never scored; refuses an existing dir):** `bash diag_coordrx.sh mooncake 20 720` (fallback `mooncake 24 600`; control `llf 20 720`), the v48 argv for `_L3_epg_rq30` with `--run-s 720`, wrapped in `py-spy record --subprocesses --gil --nonblocking` (py-spy in a SEPARATE venv `~/.venvs/pyspy`), a 5 s sampler of the fd counts of parent and child, child RSS, per-thread CPU, TCP connections to port 8031, unix-socket receive queues, vLLM running / waiting / generation counters and /health, the last decision time and request rows. Pass criteria for the proof (after the user rules on the fix): result received; request log complete; parent backlog under 100; wire-minus-decision p99 under 0.5 s; 2-token stream end near 1.5 s; then the `_FIXPROOF` rerun of mooncake n20 s4 `c3-11-2_L3_epg_rq30` against its committed values (robot accuracy 0.392, robot on-time 0.187, assistive on-time 0.890, n_requests 8,182; noise floor from the covex n16 s4 rep pair: accuracy 0.015, on-time 0.008, AP@0 0.0027, AP@1 0.0007, n_requests 0.13%), and the crashed-cell rerun completing clean.
